The active liquidity at -10 should come into play after crossing that tick. In Uniswap, a position [lower, upper) is considered active when the current price is between those two values. So, if the swap price hits exactly at tick -10, the active liquidity there would include the positions that start at -10. This seems crucial for understanding how pricing affects liquidity.\n**Evaluating tick boundaries**\n\nI'm thinking about the range from 20 to -10, and I wonder if crossing -10 affects boundary changes. If we end at -10, there might be no change at that point. The proof test shows the price limit at tick -10 as the square root price, yet it calculates the price at tick -11. So, is it problematic to start at -10? If the result must equal active liquidity at endTick, I need to check this calculation further.\n**Analyzing active liquidity behavior**\n\nI'm considering how active liquidity behaves when crossing down at tick t. Before crossing, it's active at t, but after, it might shift to t-1. The iterator code uses getNext tickNext and subsequently manipulates liquidity. If, for instance, tickNext is -10 with a liquidityNet of +10e21, subtracting leads to a zero active liquidity after crossing below -10.\n\nTo maintain active liquidity at the endTick, we need to return -10 when the endTick is -11, but not for -10. Essentially, this creates a logic for navigating ticks.\n**Clarifying tick return logic**\n\nI’m thinking about the logic of returning ticks relative to endTick.
